
## Village simulation and world economy

The pack needs recurring work for large factories, logistics networks, and late-game power plants. A village spends its treasury on food, equipment, maintenance, freight, and services it can actually use. Funded orders reserve money and receiving space. Verified deliveries settle orders and become usable stock. Industry then consumes that stock, sells its output, and creates the next round of demand. Long-lived customers and replacement programs should give players reasons to produce millions of ordinary materials after finishing the main progression.

The separately reviewed [approved village economy design](VILLAGE_AI_SIMULATION_ECONOMY.md) records newer policy, labor, debt, sovereignty and diplomacy decisions. The draft values below are working examples for discussion.

### Decisions for the next design pass

- Use one Minecraft day for daily accounting, with forecasts and routes reviewed every 1–3 real minutes as a tuning target. Stage price, demand, population, and border changes so a single recalculation cannot swing an entire economy. Slow freight should see conditions evolve during the trip while its signed price and destination stay reserved.
- Start at four food items per simulated resident per Minecraft day, within the agreed 3–5 range. At 40 million residents that means 160 million food per day. Housing, utilities, and ongoing jobs cap growth, and the district needs sustained surplus before it can grow. A nation can produce hundreds of millions across its industries and still need a separate player supply of 100 million food, components, or other shortfalls.
- Give families real housing and upkeep costs. Homes, factories, power connections, and shipyards need physical build materials such as steel and ongoing repairs. Tentatively group four residents per home and add one visible representative at 300,000 district residents and each doubling after that, up to a tested local limit around 30–40. Keep most residents in the simulation. Assign visible representatives to verified beds and workplaces.
- Reserve command-free ordinary play for the village dashboard and linked stations. The visible dashboard should open needs, multiple orders, deliveries, and territory or trade information, with the receiving barrel and network links nearby. Villages should pack output for pickup. Players can compete as individual suppliers or work cooperatively in an FTB party, with village standing averaged across members. FTB parties do not own villages.
- Connect trains and automated freight stations to nearby dashboards and verify continuous native track between endpoints. Multiple routes and distribution hubs increase actual freight capacity. Charge traction fuel or electricity and verify passenger seats, loads, and arrivals. Some coastal hubs should specialize in ships. A certified cargo or passenger route can use a ship the player actually flew at load, then reuse its proven schematic, capacity, fuel, trip duration, and range in a simulation. A nearby physical replica is a later performance and visual design question.
- Use TFMG for local machines and ElectroEnergetics for long-distance supply on the same grid. Use measured continuous and cumulative electricity, multi-site demand, maintenance, and factory operating costs to make regional power plants worthwhile. Meter power at customer boundaries. Use finished machines, machinery parts, food, and industrial replacements in recurring tenders.
- Let dry native roads and complementary stock or service deficits strongly influence which neighboring villages band together. Draw a national border when two eligible villages unite. Expansion costs materials and follows adjoining terrain, with ocean crossings reserved for trade, purchase, or negotiated territory. National cards should show public population, stability, integrity, and persistent world-seeded distinct flags. Keep exact needs in the economy interface.
- Use native veins and biomes to establish land potential. Plains support the most food, while actual output is bounded by machinery, staff, power, feedstock, storage, and freight. The simulated mine uses the known resource as a ceiling and leaves the actual vein available. Give settlements durable specialties, including farming, manufacturing, distribution, ports, and shipyards. Use regional modifiers when geology or terrain makes local extraction harder.
- Let seasons, verified ruins and dungeons, disease, bad trade, and occasional severe events affect supplies and recovery. Ruined portals can create simulated Nether raids. A verified threat can eventually obstruct territory at chunk scale and interrupt trade. Prefer repair, relief, purchased territory, or a new route before splitting a nation. Rare wars and secession should have credible power and ally checks, limited destruction or casualties, and possible reparations, recovery tenders, or stalemates. Local militia orders issue at local depots and national equipment orders at the capital or a designated hub.
- Keep Earth a long-term producer and customer. Let other worlds support distinct industries, such as Moon Desh processing, Mars pressure equipment and Ostrum, Venus refractories and Calorite, and orbital gas extraction where the progression supports it. Give each world valuable outgoing cargo and real imports. Repeated external or interplanetary customers should justify high-capacity AE2 crafting, huge inventories, and high-power generation.
- Use MineColonies Builders where they can place supplied schematics safely. Explore Hirelings for player companies, training and real output fed into existing freight, and use Villager Apprentice or custom NPC dialogue only for roles they actually support. Keep one owner for each overlapping mechanic. The tool for optional live convoys and NPC encounters needs its own performance limit.
- Treat the Warp Drive as the Part One finale. The intended cinematic leaves the solar system, shows an unknown-species warp moment, then fades to credits. Continue interstellar progression separately.
- Keep physical placement and simulation separate. A loaded chunk should read whether the village has an approved upgrade, and only a prepared placement job should do additional block work. Persist elapsed time, batch the due work, avoid forced chunks for routine economy, and measure TPS, MSPT, heap, and loaded entities before scaling.

### World and logistics design details

- Start with a circular local territory. Join only bordering villages with complementary supplies and functioning trade. Generate a limited number of starter roads, and use the strongest verified links when ranking new partners. Track sovereignty and route interruptions per chunk as the design matures, and draw clean map outlines through chunk corners. Store a nation's flag and identity across changes to ownership, including secession and replacement capitals.
- Reserve food and household services before discretionary exports. Size housing and work sites by household and job demand, and require steady surplus for growth. Give region specialists a real cost for machinery and inputs. Each manufacturing line pays the player's standard bill of materials for its products. Ports and distribution hubs gain throughput only as funded facilities and verified connections become available.
- Let nearby structures and seasons change both inputs and outcomes. Validated threats can create relief contracts, defense budgets, interrupted access, and later lost territory. A long interruption can make membership impractical even when reputation stays high. Make secession a rare last resort after relief or rerouting fails. Wars should require credible forces or allies and can end with reparations, a limited territorial transfer, or a costly stalemate. A village militia and a national force procure equipment at their own authorized depots.
- Commission freight through dashboards and nearby stations. Train verification uses real track topology and propulsion. Ships prove a route at a measured load, record their actual fuel and capacity, then may repeat it as scheduled freight. Assign passengers only where there are valid destinations and spare seats. Simulate most vehicles and passengers offscreen, and budget visible replicas separately. Cargo moves, arrives, and pays once from its original reservation even if shortages or borders change in transit.
- Give players distinct trading companies, authored NPC dialogue, and contracts that can be completed alone or with an FTB team. Treat routine villages as independent operators. Incorporate teaching, staffed depots, construction, and physical convoys where a small trial confirms the feature works with the ledger and loaded-entity budget.

### Contract and production accounting

The contract design covers repeatable tenders for food and industrial inputs, production, maintenance, freight and passengers, sustained power, bulk energy, construction, and regional exports. A shortage is the stock target minus usable stock, firm inbound shipments, earlier accepted quantities, and confirmed available local output. Lots are limited by the shortage, the depot's unreserved capacity, and the buyer's available budget. Signatures reserve that space and lock the unit price. Payment requires a matching delivery or service receipt. Repeat offers open as users consume stock and funded demand returns.

Production batches are limited by inputs at normal pack recipe costs, workers, machines, energy, and output space. Scheduled transfers can use existing hubs, with household reserves and already signed commitments first, then treaty allocations and the open market. Village and league accounts retain separate stock and treasuries. A capped regional or offworld buyer can fund recurring exports, with verifiable payments and delivery keeping local trade from paying for the same goods twice.

For the first maintenance example, a commissioned Atmospheric Scoop already needs four Steel Mechanisms and one Advanced Precision Mechanism after 32 completed extractions. With current machine settings those runs produce 32,000 mB of condensate and consume 104,857,600 FE and 51,200 mB of coolant before service. Automatically posting its missing parts, collecting the correct delivery, and applying them to that scoop remains a proposed depot integration.

### Balance examples for brainstorming

| System | Calculation or working figure |
| --- | --- |
| Population | One Minecraft day per accounting period. Each simulated resident needs four food per day, so 1 million residents require 4 million food and 40 million require 160 million. Four residents per home is the initial planning assumption. |
| Growth | Surplus food and vacant housing, utilities, and staffed capacity must coincide for three completed days. The current district model then caps growth at about 0.5% per day, also bounded by surplus food and the smallest remaining capacity. |
| Visible residents | Add one representative at 300,000, 600,000, 1.2 million, 2.4 million, 4.8 million, 9.6 million, 19.2 million and 38.4 million district residents. Available beds, verified jobs, and loaded-entity budgets also cap representation. |
| Orders | Subtract usable stock, signed incoming lots, and separately confirmed supply from the target. Round the result down to whole lots constrained by receiving space and available funds. Accepted quantities and carried freight keep their reserved price. |
| Factories | Bound output per period by equipment, labor, power, materials, terrain potential where relevant, and output capacity. Keep the full pack bill of materials for national production. |
| Industrial scale | Illustrative quotas of 33,600 steel, 1,920 Steel Mechanisms and 320 Nitro Energy Mechanisms per service day yield 1,008,000 steel, 57,600 mechanisms and 9,600 Nitro mechanisms in 30 days. Convert them to actual ore, machine, power and freight requirements before setting live quotas. |
| Territory | Track unsurveyed estimates, confirmed native ore, and current production as separate quantities. Regional capacity uses recovered yield, infrastructure, and connected terrain. Simulated output leaves players' physical veins intact. |
| Maintenance and power | Verified operating time determines spare-part demand. An eligible outage breaks a sustained-power window while preserving prior confirmed bulk energy. Measured receipt and transmission loss determine the generation requirement. |

### Installed in the live pack

- The All in one profile uses NeoForge 21.1.259 with Hirelings enabled. A full cloned pack joined a world on that loader, and the earlier dashboard revision was installed in the live profile.
- The installed revision includes a visible market stand, a Settlement Dashboard, a receiving barrel, starter bread, charcoal and plank orders, Create package intake, FTB village claim protection and team-average prices, and seasonal reserve targets. Fresh village placement uses a 64-chunk spacing grid. Water, terrain and biome restrictions can result in gaps beyond 2,000 blocks.
- The village economy revision and both targeted world-generation fixes were installed on November 14. The incompatible Forge build of Alex's Caves was moved aside so NeoForge can start. The previous, cancelled Chunky task was archived. Industrial Colonies Survival Test opened in command-disabled Survival with the exact The Pack preset, the tested terrain seed, and a snowy continental spawn. The one-line Chunky circle started at the continent center, `-134044, -93130`, with radius 100,000 blocks and a 100 GiB disk floor for a resumable pause.
- The November 14 maintenance restart installed temporary leases for already-generated village chunks, earlier road discovery, exact walking approaches, dashboard order and hazard details, and cached map geometry. Giant Trees now writes overflow outside its global capture lock and retries brief Windows file sharing conflicts. Distant Horizons' independent terrain generation is disabled during this Chunky run. Vanilla saved normally at 17,169 positions before the update. The same Survival save reopened on NeoForge 21.1.259, and Chunky resumed from that checkpoint on the original circle.

### Mods and their roles

| Status | Mod and role |
| --- | --- |
| Installed and used | RoadArchitect generates and proves starter roads. JourneyMap shows dashboard markers and MapFrontiers shows national territory and stats. Serene Seasons adjusts starter reserves. Create handles physical packages, and FTB Teams shares village reputation across members. |
| Installed, player-built roads | Via Romana charts player-built connections for fast travel. RoadArchitect is responsible for the pack's automatic starter roads. |
| Installed with scoped controls | Kingdom keeps explicit diplomacy and troops while automatic village admission, construction, road work, and siege block damage are suppressed. Villager Commerce keeps ordinary stalls and its local ledger with premium contracts disabled. Industrial Colonies owns the village contracts and national accounting. |
| Installed, available for pilots | MineColonies and Colony Logistics for builders and depots, Hirelings and Villager Industries for staffed player facilities, Easy NPC for authored dialogue, and Recruits and Caravans & Convoys for bounded encounters and routes. Create Aeronautics and Automated Logistics provide the existing ship and route mechanics. |
| Installed, separate systems | Farmer's Delight provides food. Contracts & Feasts injects its own boards with separate rewards. The Villager Industries recipe overlay targets eight ore refill routes. Player companies, Builders, and these add-on contracts still need their own settlement handoff if adopted. |
| Parked or absent | Aeronautic Crew is parked pending compatible Sable, Villager Life remains disabled, and Villager Apprentice and Pax road generation are absent from the live profile. The pack's current road provider is RoadArchitect. |

### Implemented in source and tested

- The 37-case native settlement suite passed for paid orders, escrow, physical barrel and package hand-in, repeat contracts, seasonal demand, windowed map markers, and domestic national assessments. A separate ReTerraForged world placed a dashboard with zero players online and confirmed that the selected spawn sits above sea level on a continent.
- The starter background economy settles small simulated bread and charcoal shipments across verified RoadArchitect roads. It keeps virtual stock and funds separate from items and player payments. Six native road tests passed. Advancing only the fixture clock let the production scheduler settle both commodities on five consecutive days and form a two-village nation. The saved ledger contains all five shipments and both national members. Separate funded road expansion work follows surveyed dry cardinal corridors, with logged physical or virtual lumber debits.
- MapFrontiers integration published a real frontier in the native server test. A graphical JourneyMap test opened its exact population, stability, integrity, and capital details. The latest source also includes simpler exact contour rendering, windowed dashboard markers, and assessments from completed household and domestic trade reports. The latest integrated build is still under test.
- The source also contains bounded pilots for real workshop maintenance, fuel, passengers, power metering, verified planetary freight, and certified route replay. These are individually scoped, and their current setup, verification, and supported native blocks are described in the detailed implementation notes.
- A separate 35-case native suite passed initial background dashboard placement, the exact walking-approach controls, order signing and delivery in the menu, and reporting of recorded hazard sources. It covers scheduled work, navigation, and persistence in the isolated environment. The installed .259 profile continues to need an in-world national frontier check.
- Full-pack testing found two causes of chunk delays. The ReTerraForged road adapter now discovers loaded villages and schedules native path work off the server tick. Surface validation matched the old code on 1,024 village columns and 128 additional NeoForge 21.1.259 columns, including 54 wet columns. Giant Trees now saves its ownership files atomically without waiting for the global game save queue. A legacy shard survived file-only write and reload checks.

### Open work

- Verify the installed build's natural day-by-day schedule, village alliances on generated terrain, and performance while Chunky runs. RoadArchitect writes roads during generation, so routes discovered after intervening chunks are complete still need a safe commissioning method that preserves builds. The economy requires native construction receipts before crediting those routes.
- The first live taiga market was verified with a short assisted load. The same save now contains a second taiga market about 1,024 blocks away, generated by Chunky while the player remained at spawn. Its physical dashboard and barrel were verified in saved terrain, and the matching depot has three funded listings. The districts share a surveyed border. An earlier failed RoadArchitect path is occupying one village's starter-road slot. A tested source fix releases that slot while retaining the failed road record. The native road suite passed all seven cases, including paved alternatives through existing chunks, the failed-edge recovery, and five days of trade leading to an alliance. The road changes await a live install. Trade between the two generated districts and a live alliance remain open.
- A workshop pilot can pledge part of the player's paid delivery to its maintenance depot on an exact standing contract. Source-level ledger tests passed for conservation, repeated receipts, save and restart, and cancellation. The native production-to-delivery case is staged.
- Calibrate large-volume contracts against actual production, power, costs, and logistics. Automated production jobs across many facilities, cross-nation trade, bulk offworld demand and factory-scale throughput remain open.
- Validate reliable route damage and repair evidence, per-chunk sovereignty and structure blockades, autonomous secession and defense, MineColonies or Hirelings workflows, and the cinematic. Preserve player builds and saved national identities as these behaviors are added.
- The 100,000-block circle has about 30,000 possible village placement slots before terrain filtering. The current 65,536-cell ledger can reach its cap around 1,337 dry villages with full starting territories. Before testing tens of thousands of active settlements, benchmark queue delay and save writes, then validate regional storage, compact land records, shared starter offer templates, receipt journaling and a larger persistent flag catalog.
- Initial Chunky throughput was limited by Giant Trees capture spill writes contending with the server tick. The concurrency and Windows atomic-publish fixes passed file tests. Two old tree sessions stopped on Windows file conflicts. They retain 444,417 spill files and two receipts with 256 pending captures each. The second maintenance restart saved the original Chunky circle at 25,990 positions and installed a verified in-memory queue checkpoint for ordinary autosaves, one atomic publication per spill, and bounded retries for ownership index files. Tests covered real Windows file locks and exact NBT checkpoint replacement. The installed targeted recovery command passed read-only checks with saved tree evidence. The Survival save resumed the original circle after the install and passed 29,000 positions. The first ordinary autosave wrote a readable 256-capture checkpoint. A separate slow ReTerraForged erosion tile completed without intervention. Native old-session replay and performance at scale remain open. The circle contains about 122.7 million chunks and a prior save's average terrain allocation projects to roughly 1.25 TiB at that scale, above the available space on the save drive.

## Recipe rules

- Read each card and its feedback, then check Material Processing, Personal Recipes, Approved Recipes, and the installed recipes before editing.
- For bulk edits, read the full README, progression and update logs, and prior recipe notes.
- Review Rejected first and Maybe second, and reopen Deferred only when the owner asks what an item does.
- Address rejection feedback directly, redesign blank rejections, and make a focused change to Maybe cards.
- Move new or revised cards to Progression Review, remove source copies, apply disable and restore-default decisions, and reopen Approved only when asked.
- After each review, check IDs and outputs across queues and installed recipes, and keep one working card per design.
- Log dated recipe changes, reasons, feedback, and decisions, mark unreviewed cards pending, and report feedback counts and percentages by theme per push and overall.
- Use exact IDs for canonical pack items and keep approved production routes for inputs.
- Get approval before creating custom items, adding missing prerequisites, or changing material processing, and flag unused custom items for review.
- Keep decorative parts and filler out, and choose materials and components that suit the finished item's function.
- Use Immersive Engineering components only where necessary, and reserve Create Crafts & Additions capacitors for earlier uses such as the TFMG Capacitor.
- Verify actual function, throughput, capacity, range, power draw, and unlocks before setting a recipe's tier and cost.
- Price the full upstream chain by scarcity, usable yield, batch size, machine requirements, energy, and downstream demand.
- Use the [Vein Balance workbook](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1eB8tn9jFumsnquxsZ1CsZR8GkLkyEhkbVO3lvMMpMzc/edit) and check spawn frequency, recovery, and travel separately from vein size.
- During overhauls, check demand for base materials and alloys, and use underused forms where they fit while keeping retired forms out.
- Scale selected endgame chains to millions of mined blocks and substantially higher manufacturing power.
- Keep simple conversions simple, and scale cutting yields and byproducts to the stock used.
- Cast applicable metals at 10 mB per nugget, 90 mB per ingot, and 810 mB per block.
- Give each shaped recipe an original, balanced layout based on appearance and function, avoid filler rings, and allow shared patterns for related plates and rods.
- Fill a square grid for full-block machines, and place functional parts where they belong in the finished assembly.
- Use one structural frame at most in a single-block machine, with separate load-bearing frames for large builds and non-steel supports for electronics.
- Use aluminum for Copycats and lightweight builds, brass for precision, cast iron for rigid beds, constantan for heat and sensing, and copper for electrical mounts.
- Use lead for shielding, nickel for heat and corrosion, steel for heavy loads, and zinc for light-duty supports.
- Build subassemblies from mechanisms, machines, and complex components, using the Fusion Core's subsystems as a model.
- Use finished displays such as ME Terminals in monitors and computers, cards for plug-in functions, and count CPUs already inside Computing Mechanisms.
- Use complete computers and extra chips sparingly, and choose the Basic Card or a suitable upgrade for each control function.
- Use Induction Providers for power delivery and Induction Cells for storage, with late-game designs using higher tiers.
- Reserve Mechanical Crafting for machines, including Quantum Storage blocks, and size the grid to the functional subsystems.
- Use Sequenced Assembly for parts and exceptionally complex machines, aiming for fewer than 15 purposeful operations.
- Compact physical parts and use suitable electronics processes for processors and cards.
- Use the Advanced AE Reaction Chamber for components and material processing.
- Use the AE2 Network Tool to calibrate, TFMG Screwdriver to fasten, Engineer's Hammer to form, and Create Wrench to fit Sequenced Assembly parts.
- Retain reusable deployer tools, apply supported wear to damageable tools, and consume fasteners separately.
- Mix Create, TFMG, and Mekanism as needed, and select the exact supported machine and casing tier for each operation.
- Gate each machine-family upgrade with a previous-tier subassembly, and keep each tier's first machine reachable without its own equipment.
- Check stock and alternate routes for cheaper bypasses, dependency cycles, and duplication, and use reachable in-place upgrades as the sole route for upgrade-only families.
- Price upgrades by their actual gains and put the main cost on equipment that uses a reusable setup tool.
- Keep progression order and group encased Create families where prerequisites allow.
- Keep stock Create redstone, fluid and storage interfaces, regular and smart pipes, harvesters, and ploughs, and adjust starter machines that need zinc or copper before iron.
- Gate mechanical pumps by resin yield and Engineering Processors at Industrial Iron or later.
- Base new tools on Modern Foundry materials and leave existing tool recipes in place for now.
- Use TFMG for local loads and ElectroEnergetics for continent-scale transmission in one grid, and gate each part by the capability it unlocks.
- Give each retained mechanism a reachable recipe and a functional use based on the [role and tier tables](#recipe-reference-tables), and use Logic Mechanism for Tier 6 Advanced Storage.
- Use Basic through Netherite Energy for chemical and nuclear support where appropriate, with Blazing Energy for heated stages.
- Use nuclear fuel components in Nuclear and Uranium Mechanisms, with DU Plating counting for Uranium.
- Use Industrial Colonies plating, verify fission heat sink and moderator recipes, and give RTGs 5×5 recipes with isotope, shielding, heat-conversion, and output stages.
- Gate reactors through energy demand and allow tier skipping.
- Use the Shadow Steel Press and Deployer as complexity references for the Shadow Steel Mixer.
- Use the tank balance notes for Mekanism Chemical and Dynamic Tank recipes, and match costs to capacity and throughput.
- Keep Manufactory processing focused on dust and crushed material, and disable raw-ore shortcuts and reagent conversions.
- Keep one canonical Advanced Computer and provide Advanced Peripherals Casing using existing parts.
- Start jet parts from costly existing subassemblies and scale Sequenced Assembly framing by size and performance, with mostly aluminum and steel at load points.
- Consider the Quantum Entangloporter for endgame remote transport, and keep both chunk loaders with the Spatial Anchor preferred for AE2 networks.
- Use the ME cell balancing tables and the approved 4k component, and require four previous-tier components and four finished predecessor cells in their respective recipes from 16k up.
- Scale storage-component costs exponentially with distinct higher-tier subassemblies, and raise finished item and fluid cell costs through functional parts and processing.
- Use a plastic ME Item Cell Housing without Heavy Machinery Casing, reserve it for 16k-and-up item cells, and check disassembly returns.
- Use one capacity component per crafting-storage block, with tier-appropriate Induction Providers from 1M up.
- Use Allthemodium from Refined Radiance where it fits, reserve Unobtainium for storage above 256k, and use exotic plates and composites where their properties justify the cost.
- Make quantum blocks and Infused Quantum Alloy expensive, and price 5×5 Extended AE buses by their capabilities.
- Make Entro seed, crystal, and ingot processing substantial enough to gate Concurrent Processors.
- Use Vibranium as the hardest standalone material, Allthemodium as a superconductor, and Unobtainium as a costly, versatile endgame material.
- Place all three pair alloys above Unobtainium, with Allthemodium–Vibranium for hardness and Allthemodium–Unobtainium costing more.
- Use Unobtainium–Vibranium as the hardest material and as feedstock for the specialized Quantum Infused Alloy.
- End Part One with the Interstellar Navigation Computer immediately before the Warp Drive, with Industrial Star and Black Hole as prerequisites, and reserve interstellar progression for Part Two.

### Mechanism progression tiers

Tiers mark earliest intended access. Mechanisms can serve several families.

| Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 | Tier 4 | Tier 5 | Tier 6 | Tier 7 | Tier 8 | Tier 9 | Tier 10 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Starter crafting and kinetic assembly | Seals, signals, and basic powered equipment | Early industrial drives and utility machinery | Calibrated tooling and early computerized control | Powered process control | Refined Radiance tooling and advanced automation | Advanced computing, specialized systems, and fuel handling | Reactor systems and specialized high-power equipment | Late-game Enderiam equipment and advanced power systems | Endgame equipment with specialized material requirements |
| Wooden Mechanism<br>Zinc Mechanism | Rubber Mechanism<br>Redstone Mechanism<br>Basic Energy Mechanism<br>Low Heat Mechanism | Steel Mechanism<br>Iron Energy Mechanism<br>Fluid Mechanism<br>Heat Mechanism<br>Storage Mechanism<br>Light Mechanism | Precision Mechanism<br>Golden Energy Mechanism<br>Hardened Energy Mechanism<br>Logic Mechanism<br>Experience Mechanism | Diamond Energy Mechanism<br>Advanced Fluid Mechanism<br>Advanced Heat Mechanism<br>Menril Mechanism<br>Sculk Mechanism | Advanced Precision Mechanism<br>Advanced Storage Mechanism<br>Blazing Energy Mechanism<br>Advanced Experience Mechanism<br>Physics Mechanism<br>Gyroscopic Mechanism<br>Ferricore Mechanism | Computing Mechanism<br>Uranium Mechanism<br>Niotic Energy Mechanism<br>Blazegold Mechanism<br>Source Mechanism<br>Blood Mechanism | Nuclear Mechanism<br>Netherite Energy Mechanism<br>Spirited Energy Mechanism<br>Soul Mechanism<br>Dark Mechanism<br>Void Mechanism | Enderiam Mechanism<br>Enderiam Energy Mechanism<br>Enderiam Fluid Mechanism<br>Enderiam Heat Mechanism<br>Enderiam Storage Mechanism<br>Nitro Energy Mechanism | Celestigem Mechanism<br>Eclipse Alloy Mechanism<br>Equivalence Mechanism |

## To do — current work and open design decisions

Reviewed November 9, 2026. This is the entry point for work we still want done. An unchecked item may be a design decision, pending recipe review, implementation, or an in-game verification; it does not mean nothing has been prepared. Completed work and earlier proposals remain in the design notes and chronological evidence below. Later user corrections take precedence.

### Mechanism progression and recipe overhaul

- [ ] Verify reachable recipes for all listed mechanisms against installed recipes and review cards, then resolve the equipment roles and tiers for Other and Atuned.
- [ ] Stage the Tier 6 Advanced Storage change using Logic Mechanism and validate the route without Tier 7 Computing Mechanism.
- [ ] Use the documented mechanism tiers and frame roles during the overhaul, checking material access, actual power demand, and progression bypasses before proposing recipes.

### Recent recipe and design follow-up

- [ ] Audit Sequenced Assembly cards for appropriate tools and existing unfinished workpieces, then verify the workpiece and final output icons in JEI.
- [ ] Verify the installed rod recipes after a recipe reload and review the staged sheet and plate drafts before exporting them.
- [ ] Verify the staged JEI timing and material appearance update after installation, keeping native plate textures and the canonical item IDs.
- [ ] Check the effective recipes for upgrade-only Extended AE devices and verify that the upgrades preserve the intended device behavior.
- [ ] Review pending Quantum Alloy, Quantum Storage, Quantum Crafter, and wireless equipment balance against actual function, cost, and unlock order.
- [ ] Research ElectroEnergetics devices and tier them by the improvements they bring to the shared TFMG and ElectroEnergetics power grid.
- [ ] Keep the remaining exotic alloy routes above standalone Unobtainium and finish their designs only when reopened for review.

### Reactor progression and power — current priority

- [ ] **Balance Fission → Molten Salt → Fusion through energy demand.** Earlier reactors should make manufacturing the next tier practical, but skipping a tier should remain possible. Building the fusion controller and core should demand immense energy/material production. Choose actual fabrication energy, throughput and startup/operating costs together; do not substitute mandatory earlier-reactor items for this economic progression. Numeric power balancing is not implemented.
- [ ] Audit all transitive reactor ingredients and alternate production routes for unintended hard locks or cheap bypasses. Removing the explicit controller/calibration dependencies alone does not prove every route is skippable.
- [ ] Review the current Fusion Control Unit sequence: Platinum Plate → press → circuit board, laminated contacts, Osmiridium rails, processors/computing, sensors/regulation/wiring, Iridium contacts and shielding. Export only the exact design the user approves; the installed older recipe and latest review draft currently differ.
- [ ] Verify the newly approved Pulse Power and Magnet Protection recipes reach gameplay with their mixed Brass/Railway/Radiance operations. Preserve accepted compact 3D models; rejected flat sprites remain previews only.
- [ ] Review the three revised MSR construction cards (controller, fuel cell, port) and revised shielded Fuel Reprocessor. They are drafts in Progression Review, not newly activated gameplay recipes.
- [ ] Reconcile the latest fusion-core card with the user's subsystem choices, advanced-computer direction and removal of redundant loose Allthemodium rods; inspect saved edits before changing it. Retain casing output of four.
- [ ] Rework turbine coil recipes around actual conductor/winding materials: aluminum wiring/spool for aluminum coils, appropriate conductor for gold/other coils. Verify the installed TFMG spool/winding process and its supported recipe format first.
- [ ] Retain the researched electromagnet field/power/thermal tradeoffs when balancing magnets; do not turn every branch into a simple price ladder. [Research](workflow/recipe-pass-20261029/magnet-cost-research/).

### Recipe review, exports and editor reliability

- [ ] Revisit the broad October30review pass before approval:69construction drafts and1quantum-processor machine correction passed offline validation, but the Brass Pattern Provider was explicitly judged worse. Do not describe this batch as user-approved or continue redesigning it without renewed direction after the user's stop.
- [ ] Verify the rejected-card cleanup in-game:397restore-default records were cleared;85native default recipes for67affected outputs were prepared in a late runtime restoration script. The installed-engine callback fixture passes; actual recipe reload/JEI results remain unverified. Three competing NuclearCraft fluid-tank cards moved to Disabled; runtime suppression still needs checking.

- [ ] **QIO and RTG balance.** Increase QIO costs substantially as endgame network hardware. Revisit the approved endpoints when their redesign is opened. RTG cards are parked in Maybe and need a major redesign before review, with differentiated isotope supply, shielding, heat conversion and output conditioning.
- [ ] **Refurbished Furniture power integration.** Design how Refurbished Furniture electrical items can run on the pack power grid through ElectroEnergetics or TFMG. Verify the installed mods' power interfaces and choose the connection and power costs before implementation.
- [ ] Reconcile saved review cards, approval records, exported scripts and loaded recipes. Resolve missing JEI recipe visibility separately from merely seeing “zero failed recipes” in a server log.
- [ ] Verify the crafting-table cleanup boolean fix on the next actual reload. Its installed-engine/Java predicate regression passes; that alone is not a new in-game reload result.

- [ ] Investigate archive refresh/card moves so a recipe does not vanish from the running review view when Approved is cached. Do not promise archive auto-refresh that the installed editor does not support.
- [ ] Rework the remaining user-selected machine recipes against the progression sheet, actual function and balanced cost. Approval means a custom recipe is wanted, not that it must be expensive. Preserve completed personal recipes and never silently resubmit an unchanged rejected design.
- [ ] Revisit Andesite/Brass Pattern Providers and explain Station Services Controller requirements before changing their progression or replacing their role.
- [ ] Keep rejected/default-restoration, Disabled, Maybe and Deferred meanings separate. Verify runtime suppression for explicitly disabled devices; an archive decision alone is not implementation.
- [ ] Finish pending material-processing proposals, including Crystal Binder, only after exact recipe approval. Preserve separate plate/sheet forms, canonical duplicates, authoring visibility, machine-tier gates and first-machine bootstrap.

### Storage, logistics and mass — design selected, implementation remains

- [ ] Price the ME Chest as the initial single-cell host, gate standard ME Drives substantially later and Extended ME Drives at endgame. The suggested ten-chests-versus-one-drive cost comparison is a target to evaluate, not an implemented ratio. Prevent many cheap1kcells/chests from bypassing physical storage progression.
- [ ] At the endgame storage stage, make Matter Stabilizer authorize shipboard ME Chests only; do not carry forward old Drive/Extended-Drive stabilization. Decide per-ship chest count/aggregate capacity and binding, and close nested-container/subnet/portable-cell bypasses without losing contents.
- [ ] Choose the actual cell-capacity rule. One item per byte, ordinary-vs-bulk byte weights and per-type limits were discussed but not finalized. Keep the goal clear: physical silos/vaults for large bulk, digital storage for useful mixed inventories; Storage Buses can expose physical inventories without moving them into cells.

- [ ] Implement connected Item Vault storage at thirty stacks per block and one item type across the whole structure. Resolve Item Silo parity rather than changing a shared setting blindly.
- [ ] Make loaded Vault cargo increase ship mass, counting shared multiblock inventory once and reducing mass when unloaded.
- [ ] Make Copycat material mass scale by occupied shape volume; empty Copycats remain nearly weightless. Define actual shape fractions and ship-mass integration.
- [ ] Balance late-game AE2 item/fluid cells and housings, including the first 1k tier, while retaining useful automation and physical bulk storage.
- [ ] Design and implement the selected per-cell occupied-byte power rates (1×,2×,4×,8×…), stable ranking/counting and anti-splitting rules; choose actual base/idle/tier values. Design expensive stackable late-game Energy Efficiency Cards with diminishing savings and a minimum power floor. All illustrative numbers remain provisional; power loss must not destroy contents.
- [ ] Complete loaded-cell freight restrictions and enforce physical cargo relevance. [AE2 ship limits](industrial-colonies/design/SHIP_AE2_LIMITS.md).
- [ ] Verify competing standalone tank suppression while retaining Create Fluid Tank and Fluid Vessel. Treat chemical/oxygen equipment, machine parts, digital storage and IE multiblock tank separately.
- [ ] Finish iron/aluminum sheet-metal Vault construction and decide the Silo recipe separately.

### Space, worlds and other implementation work

- [ ] Reconcile the one-navigation-computer direction, excess certification intermediates, station services and practical station/flight costs; preserve functional dependencies until replacements exist.
- [ ] Finish the Atmospheric Scoop's selected late-game 7×7 construction design and extraction prerequisites.
- [ ] Continue world-resource, finite-reserve, prospecting, terrain/structure, ships/combat and remaining implementation work from the canonical registers rather than inferring completion from historical notes.
- [ ] Reconcile processing yields, finite deposit lifetimes and factory demand; preserve the selected Earth geography/material directions. [Earth resource chart](industrial-colonies/design/EARTH_RESOURCE_MASTER_CHART.md).

### Parked for later — do not automatically reopen

- [ ] Copycat acquisition recipes; Big Reactors reactors/turbines; creative/endgame items.
- [ ] Deferred space/planet/navigation items, RTGs, batteries/voltaic piles, decay generators, bombs and other live Deferred selections. Current user judgments, not this broad family summary, control each card.

## Design notes — central reference

Updated October 29. This section gathers the design decisions recoverable from the current conversation and existing project documentation. A recorded design, saved recipe draft, approved selection, staged implementation, and installed gameplay are different states. Later explicit user decisions override older proposals; linked historical documents retain their original status and evidence. Do not infer that everything below is implemented. Add future design notes here rather than leaving them only in chronological updates.

### Storage, cargo mass, and Copycats

- **ME Chest versus Drive economics — open design:** one ME Chest should be an accessible initial one-cell host, while filling the same role with many chests should cost substantially more than a later ten-slot ME Drive. Ten chests costing around four times one Drive was an illustrative target, not a selected recipe price or guarantee. Delaying the Drive alone must not leave a cheap many-chest/1kcell workaround.
- **Physical storage integration:** ME Storage Buses can expose compatible silo/vault/chest inventory through the terminal while the contents remain physical and consume no cell bytes. This is a useful design option, not a newly implemented storage ownership rule. Keep ship cargo/mass and cross-network bypass constraints when evaluating it.

- **AE2 occupied-byte power scaling — selected October30 direction, design only:** each installed storage cell has its own ordinal multiplier: first1×, second2×, third4×, fourth8×, continuing to double. Charge the selected base energy-per-used-byte rate only on occupied bytes in that individual cell. Adding a cell does not reprice bytes in earlier cells. Sum these individual charges for the storage bank. A fourth cell with1000usedbytes would cost8000FE/t only under the illustrative1FE/used-byte/t rate; that base rate is not selected. Cell ranking/counting scope, host types, tier interaction, zero-use idle costs and stable ordering remain open. Prevent swapping/reinserting cells, stacking ME Chests or splitting networks from resetting all storage to the cheapest rank. Do not silently substitute a global multiplier on every cell.
- **Late-game Energy Efficiency Card — new design request, not implemented:** add a very expensive efficiency upgrade with stackable copies and diminishing additional savings. One card reducing consumption by50%, then a second adding only10percentage points (60%total reduction) is an example, not a fixed formula. Card name/identity, exact curve, cap, minimum power floor, placement/slot limits, recipe progression, stacking scope, interaction with cell tiers and per-cell occupied-byte multipliers still need design. More cards must never create free/negative power consumption. No recipe or upgrade behavior is implemented by this note.
- **AE2 capacity discussion — not finalized:** one item per byte, two ordinary items per byte with higher bulk costs, per-type stock limits and increasing bulk costs were explored. No final byte conversion, item-weight table or per-type capacity limit was chosen. Preserve the distinction from the selected individual-cell power direction above. Main goal remains physical silo/vault bulk storage with useful mixed-item digital access.

- **October30 storage progression:** ME Chest (one cell) starts digital storage; standard ME Drive arrives much later; Extended ME Drive is endgame. At that stage the existing Matter Stabilizer can permit shipboard ME Chests only, not either drive type. Older stabilized-drive proposals are superseded. Exact cell capacities, power, chest count/binding and byte weighting remain open. [Current ship/storage design](industrial-colonies/design/SHIP_AE2_LIMITS.md). Documentation only, not gameplay implementation.

- **Item Vault construction:** iron and aluminum sheet metal. Current pending two-vault batch retains4x3layout, two barrels and andesite end ribs; four upper iron sheets and four lower aluminum sheets. No electronics. Silo recipe unchanged pending separate design. Saved draft only, no gameplay export.

- **AE2 power continuity:** the October30individual-cell occupied-byte rule above refines the older generic exponential-power proposal. Physical Item Vaults/tanks remain passive alternatives; entry cost alone is insufficient. Exact base rate, counting scope and anti-splitting rules remain open, and power loss must not destroy stored contents.

- **AE2 digital storage — latest selected direction:** both item and fluid storage cells, their housings, and even the first 1k tier should be very late-game and expensive. Physical vaults/tanks remain useful before digital bulk storage. This updates earlier entry-tier assumptions, but does not authorize random exotic ingredients, Heavy Machinery Casing in cell housings, or automatic recipe replacement. Costs and exact progression gate still need design. User considers AE2 generally overpowered and wants a coherent balance rather than cost inflation alone.
- **AE2 balance proposal, not yet selected:** balance entry timing, storage scaling, automation convenience, and freight restrictions separately. A costly reusable housing can gate entry without repeatedly charging full infrastructure for every small capacity upgrade. Preserve physical logistics and prevent portable digital storage from bypassing ship cargo/mass limits. Do not disable useful AE automation or apply a blanket nerf without reviewing those boundaries.

- **Standalone fluid tanks — selected ownership:** retain Create Fluid Tank and Create Connected Fluid Vessel as main fluid storage; competing standalone fluid tanks go to Disabled. Existing creative tanks stay deferred for ultimate endgame. Chemical-only storage, oxygen equipment, processing tanks, digital cells and machine-component ingredients need separate treatment; do not silently erase them under a name match. Immersive Engineering's tank is a multiblock with no acquisition card in the cache and is recorded for integration review. Archive decisions do not disable runtime multiblock formation.

- **Connected Item Vaults — selected design, not implemented:** 30 stacks per constituent block; one item type across the entire connected inventory. When emptied, the vault can accept a different type. Respect actual stack limits. A maximum 3x3x9 vault would hold 2,430 stacks / 155,520 items at 64 per stack. The observed installed setting was 20 stacks per block. Silo parity is not decided: the present capacity configuration is shared, so a global change would affect both.
- **Aeronautics cargo mass — selected design, not implemented:** Item Vault mass increases with actual contents and decreases when unloaded. Add cargo mass to the craft's structural mass and lift/thrust burden. Count a shared multiblock inventory once, not once per block.
- **Copycat mass — selected design, not implemented:** empty Copycats are almost weightless. Applied-material contribution scales with the shape's occupied volume: base mass + full-block material mass times occupied fraction. A slab is approximately half a material block; a cogwheel and thin pressure plate use their own geometry. Do not count hollow volume as solid material. Exact shape fractions and mass integration remain open.
- **Copycat authoring — deferred:** all available Copycat recipe cards are parked with “Come back to these later.” Missing registered shapes with no supported acquisition recipe are inventoried, not assigned invented recipes.
- **Loaded AE storage and ship freight — existing selected direction:** local rearrangement should remain possible, while loaded cells must not bypass physical long-distance or interdimensional freight. Fixed-home/range and movement admission requirements are detailed in [Ship AE2 limits](industrial-colonies/design/SHIP_AE2_LIMITS.md). Exact range and runtime enforcement remain separate work; empty cells remain freely portable.

### Recipe balance and progression

- **Fusion Control Bearing approved:** real structural component, user-edited5x5inputs preserved, accepted six-pad inventory art. Proper approval reason is `approved`; native archive caching/new-item registration requires a fresh game start after this installation. No ordinary Diorite conversion.

- **Material fabrication and fusion:** plate/sheet/rod processing must respect machine tiers and first-machine bootstrap. Avoid generated gears; use existing copper/brass cogs where needed. Preserve accepted composite plate art. Fusion control, magnet protection and pulsed power are functional subassemblies; latest controller drafts use formed Iridium contacts rather than mandatory fission-cycle powders. Reactor progression is energy-driven and skippable; exact power balancing and transitive gate verification remain open. Installed gameplay and latest pending drafts must be reconciled before claiming parity.

- **Higher NuclearCraft plating:** Advanced/DU/Elite/Extreme recipe drafts follow Basic lead-graphite composite, same metal amounts/yields with formed reinforcing plates where appropriate; review before export. Keep NCidentities with IE-style item appearance. **NuclearCraft Chassis:** retired as a component; its recipe uses map to TFMG Steel Frame. Preserve existing-stack compatibility and verify loaded replacements.

- **Old generic intermediates versus new material parts:** retire old Industrial Colonies items whose installed model is generic vanilla gunpowder (60matched, plus prior named exceptions). New colored material dusts and all new plates/sheets/rods/gears stay; unused ones remain hidden until recipe-used. High-Purity Silicon stays glossy. Do not interpret this as blanket deletion of every dust item or a request to show unused new parts.

- **Obsolete custom powder intermediates:** user retires the named radiant/silicon precursor/lattice/PGM/iridium/graphite/silica items and the IC bearing-fraction/dust family (16IDs in retirement audit). Keep High-Purity Silicon with a glossy solid silicon appearance. Do not automatically retire unrelated high-purity metals or add substitute shortcuts.

- **Dust appearance:** grainy vanilla gunpowder/redstone-style silhouette with material-specific colors, replacing polished powder icons; do not add duplicate items. **Cinnabar intermediates:** Cinnabar Dust and Roasted Cinnabar are retired; raw Cinnabar stays. New native-dust color registration needs full client startup.

- **Duplicate component correction:** Create Golden Sheet is the canonical gold sheet; no extra custom gold sheet. Existing Allthemodium-family and Entro dusts reused. Audit must handle naming aliases, compare actual material/form, exclude decorative blocks and distinguish NuclearCraft composite tiers from ordinary metal plates. Retired custom item IDs remain hidden compatibility entries so old stacks survive.

### Nuclear, fusion, and power

- **Reactor progression — October30 correction supersedes mandatory fission locking:** Fission → MSR → Fusion should be practical energy progression. Skipping a reactor is allowed, but manufacturing the next core/controller should impose a very large energy burden on earlier power. Do not force prior-reactor items/calibration byproducts solely to enforce order. Exact energy/throughput values, whole-route costs and remaining indirect dependencies still require design and verification.
- **Fusion core structure:** preserve the user's latest shape, Allthemodium rods carrying conditioned electricity outward, side transformers, and meaningful containment/control/support roles. Do not use the most expensive magnet or block as the starter core's generic base cost.
- **Dedicated fusion subsystems — proposal accepted in direction, parked:** the central five positions could hold one Fusion Control Unit, two Magnetic Confinement Controllers, and two Power Conditioning Modules with separate meaningful recipes. Custom intermediates may be justified here; identities and recipes are not implemented.
- **Electromagnets:** price manufacturing difficulty and actual installed gameplay field/power/thermal tradeoffs, not a simplistic “every later magnet is best” ladder. Retain inexpensive basic starter magnets and 1:1 slope conversions. [Research and draft evidence](workflow/recipe-pass-20261029/magnet-cost-research/) records game statistics and manufacturing references.
- **Manufactory stays:** intended for broad physically appropriate dust/crushed processing, filling gaps in crushers. Provisional cutoff beyond Refined Radiance; explicitly exclude Allthemodium and above. Do not turn chemical reagents into arbitrary grinding recipes. Cached audit found 91 recipes and incomplete dust coverage; non-dust routes such as wafers/leather/bioplastic are flagged, not silently removed. [Coverage audit](workflow/recipe-pass-20261029/manufactory-audit/).
- **Big Reactors — deferred:** reactors, turbines and support machinery are parked; NuclearCraft covers the user's present nuclear needs. This is a review decision, not mod removal.
- **AE2 power generators — Disabled review decision:** Vibration Chamber and Crystal Resonance Generator. The former generates AE power, rather than manufacturing Fluix crystals. Runtime suppression remains separate from archive status.
- **Creative items — deferred:** potential ultimate endgame rewards, perhaps never used. All available cards parked; no unsupported acquisition recipe is invented for creative-only entries.

### Space, computers, and endgame materials

- **Orbital Flight Computer:** belongs just after Refined Radiance, retaining advanced computers and navigation processors. Remove endgame ATM alloys, Ultimate Control Circuits, Ultimate Energy Cubes, and Industrial Star requirements. Current review draft uses plastic covers, Advanced circuits, ordinary capacitors, and a Refined Radiance casing. User may park it as Maybe; latest live judgment controls.
- **Certification documents/custom items:** Guidance- and Propulsion-Certified Plans are construction intermediates, not standalone machines. User is deferring the custom-plan group and questions excess custom intermediates; do not add more without a meaningful manufacturing purpose.
- **Atmospheric Scoop:** functional gas-giant condensate harvester requiring an operational extraction-region station, power, coolant, and output capacity. User wants late-game 7x7 construction cost; do not confuse this request with an already completed recipe change.
- **Gas giant extraction and storage (planned)**
  - **Atmospheric yields:** Saturn produces slightly more ordinary hydrogen than Jupiter. Jupiter is the helium-focused destination. Ammonia and ethane are trace constituents of both atmospheres. Atmospheric collection can recover ammonia using the existing NuclearCraft fluid. The pack can optionally represent collected ethane with its existing ethene fluid.
  - **Deep extraction:** Both planets can yield custom Liquid Metallic Hydrogen from deep extraction, with Jupiter yielding substantially more than Saturn. This is a separate resource from ordinary hydrogen gas and cryogenic liquid hydrogen.
  - **Containment:** Transfer Liquid Metallic Hydrogen directly into an Ultimate-tier tank. Only that tank tier accepts it. Ordinary tanks and buckets reject it. Loss of containment converts it to ordinary hydrogen gas. The deep extraction route has no separate cryogenic conversion step.
  - **Unobtainium demand (proposed):** Use a large ordinary-hydrogen cost at the Pressurized Reaction Chamber to give bulk gas collection and transport a late-game purpose. Ten million units was a discussion target. Confirm throughput, per-operation capacity, and whether ordinary production can bypass gas giant collection before setting the cost. If the shard step is adopted, hydrogen chloride can remain the Chemical Injection Chamber reagent.
  - **Open balance values:** Set collection rates, atmospheric gas recovery ratios, and the final hydrogen demand after testing storage and transport.
- **Exotic pair alloys:** no generic price tags for earlier machinery/ordinary capacity upgrades. Purification, losses/slag, meaningful intermediates and separate final activation must justify their advanced routes. Preserve distinct pair-alloy difficulty and current protected/parked recipes.
- **Crystal Binder:** use the existing NuclearCraft binder for meaningful endgame joining/composite matrices, directly or through components. Do not invent a duplicate binder or add it redundantly to every final machine. [Chemistry direction](industrial-colonies/design/ENDGAME_BINDER_AND_CHEMISTRY.md).
- **Antimatter before quantum:** SPS access must precede quantum materials and any alloys needing antimatter activation. Avoid SPS/alloy circular dependencies; preserve the user's latest SPS parts and Supercharged Coil sequence.

## Item Vault capacity and single-item rule — design decision, October 29

User changes the Item Vault target to 30 stacks per physical vault block, with ONE item type shared across the entire connected multiblock, not one independent type per constituent block. The connected inventory should accept more of its current item type and reject other types while occupied; once completely empty, it may accept a different type. Capacity remains stack-based and respects the accepted item's stack limit. For ordinary 64-stack items, each block contributes 1,920 items; a maximum 3x3x9 (81-block) vault contributes 2,430 stacks / 155,520 items.

This supersedes the observed 20-stack-per-block capacity as the requested design target. The current Create capacity setting is shared with Create Connected's Item Silo, so increasing it globally would affect both; the user's single-item decision here explicitly concerns connected Item Vaults. Vault-specific enforcement and any intended silo parity must be handled deliberately. No config or runtime code changed in this documentation step. The one-type restriction is not provided merely by increasing capacity. Preserve the separate cargo-dependent Aeronautics mass requirement, counting shared contents once.

## Item Vault cargo mass for Create Aeronautics — design note, October 29

An Item Vault on a Create Aeronautics craft should become heavier as more cargo is loaded into it. Total vault mass consists of its empty structural mass plus the mass of its stored contents. Cargo should contribute to the craft's total mass and therefore its handling and lift/thrust requirements. An empty vault must not weigh as much as a full one; unloading removes the corresponding cargo mass. Count the actual stored item quantities and their material/item mass rather than a fixed mass per occupied inventory slot. Do not count the same shared multiblock inventory once per vault block.

This records the user's requested design direction. Exact item-mass mappings and physics integration remain implementation work; no current runtime behavior is asserted or changed. Task-register completion counts remain unchanged.

## Copycat material-dependent mass — design note, October 29

Copycat blocks and items should have a very small, nearly negligible empty weight. Once a material is applied, their mass should reflect that material scaled to the actual volume represented by the copycat shape, rather than automatically inheriting a whole block's mass.

Proposed rule: total mass = light empty-copycat mass + (applied material's full-block mass × occupied material volume as a fraction of one block).

A slab represents approximately half a block of its material; a gold copycat slab therefore contributes approximately half a gold block's mass, plus its small base mass. A thin pressure plate contributes only its thin plate volume. A gold copycat cogwheel uses its own gear-shaped volume, not a whole gold block or a slab's fixed fraction. Different applied materials must therefore give the same copycat shape different weights. Empty spaces do not count as filled material. Where multiple material regions are supported, sum their separate material-volume contributions without double-counting.

Exact per-shape fractions, treatment of tiny details, and the integration with the pack's physics/mass system remain implementation work. This records the user's design direction only; no mass behavior, recipes, or gameplay files were changed. Existing task completion counts remain unchanged.

## Original yield table restored to the entry point

Copied unchanged from the retained October19 README history. These are the recorded design values and pending proposals, not proof of current installed recipes. No recipe or temperature changes made. Historical arithmetic is preserved rather than silently corrected.

### Production yield table - ore, output and molten recovery

Each row starts with one ore/rock. Paired amounts are normal / rich. **10 mB per processed crushed item or ore-derived dust is the latest best-recovery baseline; balance rarity mainly through crushing quantities.** Explicit exceptions remain chromium's four-dust batch, rich osmium/platinum at45mB, finished gold dust at90mB, and gold flakes at0.5mB. The user permits case-by-case melt exceptions when needed. A draft is proposed, not an approved or exported recipe. Machine assignments marked pending are not claims of native support.

**Units: nugget10mB, ingot90mB, storage block810mB (nine ingots).** Fireproof iron recovery produces **iron**, never automatic steel; steel is a separate later recipe. Preserve the agreed rich-chromium four-dust quantity rather than silently changing it to nine. Bauxite stays TFMG Bauxite Powder; it is not finished aluminum dust.

| Ore input | Crushing / collection | Amount per input | Seared Smelter output | Blast Furnace output | Fireproof Vat / final recovery |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Magnetite / Rich Magnetite | Crushed Raw Iron | 1 / 3 | 3 / 9 mB iron | Separate steel recipe, later | Fireproof Vat: 10 mB iron/output; 10 / 30 mB per ore |
| Hematite / Rich Hematite | Crushed Raw Iron | 5 / 9 | 15 / 30 mB iron | Separate steel recipe, later | Fireproof Vat: 10 mB iron/output; 50 / 90 mB per ore |
| Sphalerite / Rich Zinc | Crushed Raw Zinc | 6 / 12 (draft) | Not assigned | Not assigned | Heated Steel Chemical Vat: 10 mB zinc/output target; 60 / 120 per ore |
| Chalcopyrite / Rich Copper | Crushed Raw Copper | 6 (draft) / 10 | Not assigned | Not assigned | 10 mB copper/output target; 60 / 100 per ore; machine pending |
| Bauxite / Rich Bauxite | TFMG Bauxite Powder | 6 / 12 | No direct ore melting | Not assigned | Heated Steel Chemical Vat, centrifuge + side electrodes; 10 mB aluminum/powder target; CO2 byproduct amount pending |
| Galena / Rich Galena | Crushed Galena | 3 / 6 (draft) | Not assigned | Not assigned | 10 mB lead/output target; 30 / 60 per ore |
| Cassiterite / Rich Tin | Crushed Raw Tin | 2 / 4 (draft) | Not assigned | Not assigned | 10 mB tin/output target; 20 / 40 per ore |
| Pentlandite / Rich Nickel | Crushed Raw Nickel | 2 / 4 (draft) | No direct ore melting selected | Not assigned | 10 mB nickel/output target; 20 / 40 per ore; machine pending |
| Argentite / Rich Silver | Crushed Raw Silver | 1 / 2 (draft) | Not assigned | Not assigned | 10 mB silver/output target; 10 / 20 per ore |
| Gold ore / Deepslate Gold ore | Crushed Raw Gold | 1 (draft) | Not assigned | Not assigned | 10 mB gold/output target |

## October 24 — current recipe and progression decisions

This section consolidates the latest voice review. It records design intent separately from saved cards and installed behavior.

- **Exotic alloy consumers:** Allthemodium–Vibranium and the other pair alloys must not be generic price tags in earlier machines or ordinary capacity upgrades. The advanced pattern-provider capacity upgrade is the user's example. Review consumers together, then change only where actual function and progression justify it. Quantum/field machinery may justify these alloys. Five active ATM–Vibranium consumers are inventoried in `workflow/exotic-alloy-consumer-policy-20261024/DESIGN_NOTE.json`; the exact named upgrade has not yet been located in saved project outputs. No blanket consumer edits or requeues have been made.
- **Alloy processing remains unfinished:** dust straight to finished alloy is rejected. Meaningful intermediates, purification, explicit M-X losses and slag removal must precede a separate final activation yielding one ingot. A chemical vat must not directly provide the final activated alloy. ATM–Vibranium may use antimatter at the finish; ATM–Unobtainium needs a different, costlier route; Vibranium–Unobtainium is the most demanding campaign-scale alloy. This is not a literal month-long recipe timer. Do not use unrelated alloy ingots as arbitrary carriers or duplicate Crystal Binder producers.
- **Intermediate exception under discussion:** the user rejected merely disguising Dirty Vibranium Dust as dirty pair-alloy dust. A real custom intermediate may be appropriate here; exact item identities and scope have not been selected or registered. Molten, dirty slurry, dirty dust and raw material ideas are exploratory, not authorization to invent fluid IDs or silently replace the chain.
- **Antimatter before quantum:** the SPS must be buildable before quantum technology and before the alloys whose activation needs antimatter. The circular SPS Port ATM–Vibranium input was replaced with an Allthemodium block. The user accepted that block and does not want more ATM added to the port merely for cost. Quantum containment plates were replaced with pre-quantum carbide materials. Later player edits to port/casing are authoritative and must not be overwritten with older snapshots.
- **SPS construction:** no separate SPS controller exists. Dedicated parts are casing, port and Supercharged Coil; compatible glass can form shell sections. The casing should have a square, coherent layout. The user edited its rods, carbide, platinum, casing and Radiance arrangement. The port uses two SPS casings, high-tier processors/control circuits and Steel Smart Fluid Pipes at the edges; early TFMG boards are too cheap. Avoid charging separately for Radiance already supplied through its casing without a functional reason.
- **Supercharged Coil:** build on an SPS casing, deploy the laser first, then install superconducting BSCCO coils, nuclear pellets, conditioned power and controls. Preserve the user's latest magnetic-alloy sheets, electrum rod and per-step Brass/Industrial Iron/Radiance/Shadow Steel machine selections. The latest saved user card has fourteen stages, including two final Shadow Steel press stages. Do not silently normalize that edit or mark its exact later form approved from an older hash. Casing/port approval hashes match their snapshot; the coil has a later edit than its recorded approval.
- **Leacher retained, not disabled:** use it as finite vein/node extraction and the sole proposed Earth extraction route for Vibranium. ATM and Unobtainium remain manufactured, not directly mined finished metals. Ordinary resource slurry chains should feed useful existing refining infrastructure rather than obsolete earlier machinery. Each material needs appropriate acid, time and native energy costs, with Vibranium especially demanding. These are designs, not implemented routes.
- **Leacher area and backfill:** reuse already established node geometry; only Vibranium's node still needs definition. Native map mode scans one chunk below the machine; research-paper mode depletes a finite abstract reserve. Desired area upgrades require new behavior and a hard cap based on existing node dimensions. Confirmed backfill rule: consume one approved replacement block per physical block extracted; hold before removal when backfill is unavailable. Abstract reserve depletion is not physical block removal, so do not invent cavities or backfill consumption for that mode.
- **Wasteland/anomalies:** installed NuclearCraft has both a Wasteland dimension and biome. Natural anomaly spawning is dimension-restricted. Resolving anomalies can produce configurable shards and marks their location destroyed. Their drops are candidates for meaningful exploration gates; resolution methods, exact drop settings and renewability still require investigation. No anomaly-based recipe has been selected or saved.
- **Immediate priority:** return to Material Processing Review and finish the exotic/alloy proposals, then early survival and world/structure readiness for starting a world soon. Do not promise readiness within an hour. The register still records first-tool, first-heat, Zinc/Brass, Foundry/bootstrap and world-generation prerequisites; these are existing unresolved entries, not newly reproduced defects. A desired play date does not authorize production installation, gameplay export or save edits.
- **Review boundaries:** preserve Personal Recipes, original Material Processing references, user edits and approval history. Processing belongs in Material Processing Review, construction in Progression Review. Use the proposal gate; no unchanged repeated cards, ledger resets or cosmetic requeues.

Overall **204/289 (70.6%)**; progression **33/79 (41.8%)**. This documentation reconciliation is **1/1 (100%)**, with no master-task completion credit. Three final alloy activation designs remain unsaved. No production installation, gameplay export or push was performed.
